and flag the
{\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}
configuration.

We now give the proof of correctness for the ghost-read module.

Proof: Algorithm of
Fig.

10
.

We claim that for a register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
,
{\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}\oplus q_{\mathtt{init}}
iff
there exists an initialized run
\rho\in{{\mathtt{Runs}}}\left({\mathcal{M}}\right)
,
\rho=\rho^{\prime}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
, and, there is no event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta,x,a,\mathtt{v}\right)}\in\mathtt{Events}
, such that
\rho=\rho_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot\rho_{1}
, where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
is outputted on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}

\Rightarrow
To prove the algorithm does not calculate false negatives, we claim the following:

If
\left\langle a\right\rangle\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}(q)
, then there exists a run
\rho
, starting from
q
such that the value of
a
is outputted upon some read event at the end of
\rho
.
We will prove this by induction on the number of rules that have been applied (number of steps in the algorithm).

•

Base case
\#steps=1
.
Since we start with no registers associated with any states, the only update that can take place is
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
.
For this rule to be applied, we must be at a state
q
that can output the value of a register within
1
step, and those are the ones where there exists edge
q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}}q^{\prime}
in
\Delta
of
{\mathcal{M}}
. Thus, based on
Fig.

10
,
\left\langle a\right\rangle\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}(q)
and
\rho={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
.

•

Inductive hypothesis: the statement holds after
n
steps of the algorithm.

•

Inductive step:
On step
n+1
we are possibly able to apply any of the rules in
Fig.

10
.
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
has already been covered and thus we examine the following cases:

Rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
:
This rule does not update the registers associated to a state, thus from inductive hypothesis the claim holds.

Rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
:
We see that in order to apply this rule it must be the case that for the previous iteration of the algorithm
\left\langle b\right\rangle\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}(q^{\prime})
, and there exists edge
q\xrightarrow{a:=b}q^{\prime}
in
\Delta
.
We see that if
b
was able to be outputted after a run
\rho
, then it will still be able to be outputted after the run
\rho
, with
a:=b
, and the statement holds.

Rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
:

Very similarly as above, we see that the value of
a
is not replaced with
b
and thus the value of
b
will be able to be outputted after
\rho
, starting from
q
.

Rules
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
,
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
and
\mathop{\hbox to8.92pt{\vbox to8.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45879pt\lower-4.45879pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 C 5.89 3.25 3.25 5.89 0 5.89 C -3.25 5.89 -5.89 3.25 -5.89 0 C -5.89 -3.25 -3.25 -5.89 0 -5.89 C 3.25 -5.89 5.89 -3.25 5.89 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.45001pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
:
It is clear that the transitions studied here do not affect the fact that the value of a register
b
can be outputted after the run guaranteed to exist from the inductive hypothesis, extended at the beginning by the relative event. Thus the claim holds.

We now clearly have that if
{\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}\oplus q_{\mathtt{init}}
there exists a run
\rho
that, starting on
q_{\mathtt{init}}
, will output the value of
a
.
However since
q_{\mathtt{init}}
is where the register machine starts its computation with an empty set of registers, we have that
{\mathcal{M}}
would allow this run to take place even when
a
is unitialized, and the statement holds.

\Leftarrow
To prove the reverse direction, i.e. that the algorithm detects all violations, we claim the following:

If there exists an initialized run
\rho\in{{\mathtt{Runs}}}\left({\mathcal{M}}\right)
,
\rho=\rho^{\prime}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
, and, there is no event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta,x,a,\mathtt{v}\right)}\in\mathtt{Events}
, such that
\rho=\rho_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot\rho_{1}
, where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
is outputted on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
, then
{\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}\oplus q_{\mathtt{init}}
.

To prove this we have: we know that since
\rho=\rho^{\prime}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
then for some
q\in Q
,
q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}}q^{\prime}
is in the
\Delta
of
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Thus clearly, from Rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
,
\left\langle a\right\rangle\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}(q)
.
Since
q
is reachable by
q_{\mathtt{init}}
through
\rho
, we need to see what sort of edge would be the last one on the path connecting them.
Depending on what kind of edge is the last on the path we can check the rules of
Fig.

10
and determine how it would propagate backwards the registers associated with the current state.
We do so and see that the only way that the claim does not hold, is if the transitions of
Fig.

10
are halted from propagating the register
a
backwards to
q_{\mathtt{init}}
.

We see that this would only happen if either there is an input on
a
, or the value of some other register
b
is written on
a
.
Of these two we know that the first is impossible due to the premise of the statement we are proving, and thus we see that now we are left in the same situation, but for register
b
, and with a shorter run left between the current state that marks
b
as dangerous, and the initial state that the remaining of
\rho
leads to.

Since the premise of the statement forbids from any input writing on the register that is the current register at any point we know that some register will end up being associated to
q_{\mathtt{init}}
, and thus trigger the rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

∎

D.2
Mismatched Variables

In this part we tackle the problem of a register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
outputting the value of a register in a variable other than the variable that the register was written for.
The algorithm is very similar to that for ghost reads, only now we need to keep track of extra information regarding the variable whose value was stored in a register.
For this violation to occur we still need a output event to take place, and thus we will initialize the search from output edges in
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Fig.

12
summarizes the update rules for the data structure used by the algorithm.

Figure 12
:
The inference rules for updating the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}
data structure , for each state
q\in Q
of a register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
during the mismatched variable reads module. We assume that
x\neq y
and
b\neq a
. A
-
in a tuple means that that particular attribute is of no importance to the rule, but will be carried over in the update.

Our algorithm works as follows:

•

It uses a data structure
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}}:{Q}\rightarrow{2^{\mathtt{Regs}*{\mathcal{V}}}}
.
If
\left\langle a,x\right\rangle\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{regs}}}(q)
then there is a run
\rho
of
{\mathcal{M}}
from
q
, where whatever was stored in
a
at
q
will be outputted to variable
x
.

•

The algorithm first isolates all output edges
E_{out}
of the register and then searches backwards through the edges or
{\mathcal{M}}
. We update the data structure as
Fig.

12
indicates.

•

If at any point a state marked with
\left\langle a,x\right\rangle
can be reached by a
q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta,a,y,-\right)}}
transition , it means that the value of
y
will be stored in
a
, but later on a run can continue and output it on
x
. We assume the register machine is able to reach all states, which means this is a violation.

In
Appendix

D
we give an example of a register machine that will flag an
{\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}
configuration in this module. The run
\rho=q_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{1},x,a_{1},-\right)}}q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{2},x,a_{1}\right)}}q_{2}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{2},y,a_{2},-\right)}}q_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{1},x,a_{1},-\right)}}q_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{2},x,a_{1}\right)}}q_{2}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{1},x,a_{2}\right)}}q_{3}
outputs the value of
a_{2}
on
x
, even though during
\rho
it inputted in
a_{2}
on
y
.

We now proceed similarly with the algorithm for Question
1
. The proof is very similar to the previous one. I.e. we need to show that 1) our rules indeed mark and propagate register-variable pairs correctly, and 2) the rules mark all possible dangerous register-variable pairs per state.

Proof: Algorithm of
Fig.

12
.

The proof follows the same structure as above. We can use induction on the number of steps to prove that associating a variable-register tuple to a state preserves the premise, and for the reverse it suffices to check that no valid tuple is stopped from propagating backwards when the relevant path exists.
∎

From now on we assume that the algorithms of
Fig.

10
an
Fig.

12
have been run in advance, and thus the register machines we are checking for cyclic
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}_{x}}}}}
relations have successfully passed those checks.

Appendix E
Proofs of WRA algorithm

Here we will prove the correctness of the algorithms in
Section

3.2
.
Before proving the final algorithm, we will prove the following lemma:

Lemma 13
.

For register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
and run
\rho\in{{\mathtt{Runs}}}\left({\mathcal{M}}\right)
, with
\left\langle\gamma_{\it init},G_{\emptyset}\right\rangle\xRightarrow{\rho}\left\langle\gamma_{1},G\right\rangle
, such that
\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}}\right]}\,\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}
is cyclic in
G
, then there exist
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
, such that
\rho=\rho_{1}\cdot\rho_{2}
and
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, and for any prefix of
\rho_{0}
, the corresponding execution graph is acyclic on
\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}}\right]}\,\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}
for all
x
, and
\left\langle\gamma_{\it init},G_{\emptyset}\right\rangle\xRightarrow{\rho_{1}}\left\langle\gamma^{\prime},G_{1}\right\rangle
, with
G_{1}\not\models{\mathtt{WRA}}
.

Proof.

To prove this we observe the rules of
Fig.

3
. We clearly see that if a prefix
\rho_{t}
of the run
\rho
the corresponding execution graph
G_{t}
contains no cycles and the next event in
\rho
is a write or a copy, then the updates that will take place on the graph do not create any cycles.
Thus the only way to create one is by adding an appropriate read event.
Since we start from the empty execution graph
G_{0}
, witch of course does not contain cycles, as it contains no events and all the relations are empty, we just define as
\rho_{1}
the first prefix of
\rho
for which a cycle is formed (since one has to exist in order for
G
to contain one. Thus the promise holds for
\rho_{1}
.
∎

In what follows when studying a run
\rho
for which the corresponding execution graph contains a cycle for
\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}}\right]}\,\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}
for some
x
, we will assume that
\rho=\rho_{0}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, and the execution graph corresponding to
\rho_{0}
has no cycles.
For such a violating run we state the following:

Proposition 14
.

Assume a register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\not\models{\mathtt{WRA}}
. Then, there exists a differentiated run
\rho\in{\mathcal{M}}
, where

•

\rho=\rho_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot\rho_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{1},x,\mathtt{v}\right)}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{2},x,\mathtt{v}\right)}
(possibly
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}
), and

•

\left\langle\gamma_{\it init},G_{\emptyset}\right\rangle\xRightarrow{\rho}\left\langle\gamma_{1},G\right\rangle
,
where
G=\left\langle E,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}\right\rangle
, and
\gamma_{1}=\left\langle q,{\mathcal{R}}\right\rangle
, and

•

\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}}\right]}\,\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}
contains a cycle

Intuitively, this states that two things need to happen in order for a cycle to occur.

•

A
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
needs to occur in some thread
\theta_{1}
.

•

This
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
then must not available for a thread
\theta_{2}
to read due to other events that have occurred already.

•

The value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
needs to be outputted for a read event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
after said “hiding” on
\theta_{2}
.

We now focus on the second condition of the above list, i.e. we will try to see the exact situation upon which a given
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
is not available to be read by a thread, because a cycle would be created. We refer to
Fig.

3
, which shows us what kind of updates would take place in a graph where this forbidden read event takes place. In this case
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
. There we see:

•

The updates to the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}
relations cannot create a cycle, as the previous execution graph is acyclic (not that
\rho
is assumed to be minimal), and the added edges add one-directional edges between the existing graph and the added new node for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
.

•

The cycle that is created upon updating the execution graph must have come from the update rule for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}
, which means that there must be at least on more
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}
in
\rho
, on variable
x
that satisfies the relevant update condition.

We therefore obtain one extra piece of information for this minimal, differentiated, violating run
\rho
, and this is that it contains another write event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}
on variable
x
. We now focus on determining which of the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}
precedes the other in
\rho
.

We assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}
is earlier in
\rho
.
Since
\rho
is minimal and thus there are no cycles in
\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}}\right]}\,\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{hb}}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}
until the event
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
occurs.
Therefore, we have that the addition of the
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}})
that the the update rule for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}
dictates would not cause a cycle (as all old and added edges are pointing in a consistent time-order towards the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
.

Thus it must be the case that
\rho=\rho_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot\rho_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, and
\rho_{2}=\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}
.
The naming here aims to relate the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{exposed}}}
tuples that will be produced from Fig.
5
to the
\rho_{\beta}
part of the run, and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{fragile}}}
to the
\rho_{\alpha}
part.
Based on this partition of
\rho
and the fact that the
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
event triggers an update of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}
that cases a cycle, we state the following:

Proposition 15
.

Assume a register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\not\models{\mathtt{RA}}
. Then, there exists a minimal differentiated run
\rho\in{\mathcal{M}}
, where

•

\rho=\rho_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\cdot\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{1},x,\mathtt{v}\right)}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{2},x,\mathtt{v}\right)}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{3},x,-\right)}
(possibly
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}=\theta_{3}
), and

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, and

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}}^{-1}\right]}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}

We now prove a short lemma regarding where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is stored at.

Lemma 16
.

For any state
q
of the register machine
{\mathcal{M}}
, that was accessed during
\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}
, we have that there is some tuple in one of the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{fragile}}}\text{ or }{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{exposed}}}
data structures of
\left\langle q,q^{\prime}\right\rangle
, with contents
\left\langle-,c\right\rangle
where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
was in register
c
when
q
was accessed.

We prove this for the rules of
Fig.

5
.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is relatively straightforward. We see that the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
is initially at register
a
. The only reason this will change while traversing
\rho
backwards is either a copy command, or a input on this (or the current) register.

In the case of a copy command we see that indeed the register identifying the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{fragile}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{exposed}}}
tuples that the state is assigned to are updated accordingly through rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, which handles all possible ways that the value of
a
would have originated in a different register.

In the case of an input command on the current register
c
we see that now the value of that is outputted in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta,x,a\right)}
is no longer in
any
register, and we observe that accordingly no rules propagate a tuple to the backwards reachable state.
∎

In essence the above lemma captures that the rules of
Fig.

5
maintain at least as much information as those of
Fig.

12
.
We highlight that in all the above discussions we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
must reading from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
.
This should be clear as it is the main assumption for the existence of the violating run and the relevant run fragments.
Therefore we can safely assume, combining this with
Lemma

16
that throughout
\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}\cdot
any activated transition is never writing (by input) a new value to the current register stored at the tuples of the state reached by the transition.

Corollary 17
.

For the above violating run and partition:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
is the latest (rightmost) input on the register that the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is stored at during that event of the run.

•

All input events after
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}
in
\rho
target registers different than the one where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is stored at during that event of the run.

We make the following claims:

Lemma 18
.

Let
\rho
be the run of
Proposition

15
, and
q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathtt{o}}}q^{\prime}
be a copy edge accessed during the
\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}
part of
\rho
.
Then,
tuple\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{status}}}(q^{\prime},q^{\prime\prime})
implies
tuple\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{status}}}(q,q^{\prime})
, with all the values that the registers associated to
q^{\prime}
carried being now stored at the registers associated to
q
, in the respective slots.

Proof.

This proof is relatively straightforward. Note that due to
Lemma

16
, the registers
c
and
b
are guaranteed to be exactly the registers where the value that will be outputted in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is currently stored at.
We proceed with case analysis on the form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathtt{o}}
.

•

if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathtt{o}}
overwrites the value of
b
, with
b
the register holding the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
in
\rho
. We see the update that will take place in this case is rule
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
.
The update to the current register for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is correct and the premise holds.

•

if
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathtt{o}}
overwrites the value of
a
, where
a
held the value of some other register that will be outputted later on on the run
\rho
towards
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, then similarly, the activated rule is
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, which update only the relevant tuples accordingly, but not the register for the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
, and the statement holds.

•

The copy edge overwrites the value of a register that is neither part of the tuple associated to
q^{\prime}
, or the register where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is stored at.

We see that the only rule applied then is
\mathop{\hbox to8.48pt{\vbox to8.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.24052pt\lower-4.24052pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.59 0 C 5.59 3.09 3.09 5.59 0 5.59 C -3.09 5.59 -5.59 3.09 -5.59 0 C -5.59 -3.09 -3.09 -5.59 0 -5.59 C 3.09 -5.59 5.59 -3.09 5.59 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.12503pt}{-2.57777pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, which correctly does not update any of the registers, but does assign the claimed tuples to
q
.

∎

We now have that all copy edges have been proven to soundly propagate whatever information was stored in some state regarding registers, to new states.
We combine the above lemma and
Corollary

17
to obtain:

Corollary 19
.

Let
\rho
be the run of
Proposition

15
, and
q
be a state accessed during the
\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}
part of
\rho
.
If
a
is the secondary register in of any
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{status}}}
tuple, then
a
is not the register where the value of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}
is currently stored at.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$,wehaveanidenticalargumentwhichisthusomitted.\qed\end@proof\par\par\par\par\par\par Togeneralizeevenmoretheabovewehave:\par\par\begin{lemma}Let $\rho$ be the run of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:splitting_violating_run}, and $q\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left(\theta,-,-,-\right)}}q^{\prime}$ be an input or output edge that is accessed during the $\rho_{\beta}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}\cdot\rho_{\alpha}$ part of $\rho$,
and the value that will be outputted on ${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ is stored in $c$.
Assume that $q^{\prime}$ is followed by several events which belong in $\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ paths in the execution graph $G$ coresponding to $\rho$.
Then the following hold:

if ${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathtt{o}}$ is an input on a new thread, but the value that is inputted has been later on read by an event in one of the threads in one $\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ path.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@${q}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{status}}}}\left(a\right)\left(b\right)\left(x\right)\right]}\,{q^{\prime}}$ is added if the set of threads that can reach the event ${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ though a
$\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ path does not chance from $q^{\prime}$ to $q$, and the value currently in $b$ will be outputted later on in one of the threads that are still in a $\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{r}}}$ path.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$wouldhavebeenappliedearlieronwhensuchoutputsoccurred.\par Bythecorrectnessofthepropagationsthatweprovedearlierin\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:path_implies_tuple},wehavethatatuple${q}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathsf{exposed}}}}\left(a\right)\left(a\right)\left(x\right)\right]}\,{q^{\prime}}$wouldbeavailablebythetimewereachthe${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}$,andthiswouldmarkthe${\tt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Unsafe}}$.\par\underline{Hypothesis:all$\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}$pathsupto$n$lengtharemarked.}\par\underline{Step:Wetakeonemorestepbackwards.}\par Wecheckthe$\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}\cup{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}\right]}\,^{*}$paththatmustconnectusto${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}{\mathsf{w}}}^{\prime}$.Sinceweneedtobeinanunmarkedthread,thispathcannotstartfroma${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{po}}}}$edge(duetohypothesis).Similarly,ifitstartsfroma${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}$edgewefallinasimilarscenarioastheeventthatthe${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{rf}}}}$istargetedcannotbemarked.\par Thereforethepathmuststartfroma${\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{{co}}}}$edge,inwhichcaseweperformananalysissimilartothebasecasewedidandwearedone.\qed\end@proof Theaboveestablishesthecorrectnessof\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{algo:wra_pseudo}andthusprovesProposition\ref{prop:algo_RA_three_parts}.\par\par\par\par\par\par\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Hardness proofs}
\par\par We start by giving the construction for the {$\mathsf{coNP}$}~hardness reduction.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\coNP~hardness}
We reduce from the problem of Tautology, i.e. given a boolean formula, decide whether it is a tautology or not.
Without loss of generality we assume $\varphi$ is in $3$-disjunctive normal form, and we will represent its clauses as $c_{0},c_{1},\ldots c_{n-1}$, and the literals occurring in a clause $i$ as $\ell_{i,1},\ell_{i,1},\text{ and }\ell_{i,3}$. Hence:
\par$$\varphi=c_{0}\vee c_{1}\vee\ldots\vee c_{n-1}=(\ell_{0,1}\wedge\ell_{0,2}\wedge\ell_{0,3})\vee\ldots\vee(\ell_{n-1,1}\wedge\ell_{n-1,2}\wedge\ell_{n-1,3})~.$$\par The reduction works as follows:
\par\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@Given the formula $\varphi$, we parse it and create a register machine over: \begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@The threads $\theta_{i}$ with one thread per clause of $\varphi$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@A single variable $x$, and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@the registers $a_{i}$ and $b_{i}$ corresponding to a clause $c_{i}$ being satisfied or violated.
\end{enumerate}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@We construct three phases in the register machine. The three phases are, in the order we describe:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@The initialization of clauses phase, where the register machine performs $2$ write events per clause $c_{i}$ of the formula $\varphi$, in a single path,
$$q_{2i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{i},x,b_{i-1\mod n},\right)}}q_{2i+1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{i},x,a_{i},\right)}}q_{2i+2}$$\par starting at state $q_{0}$, and ending in state $q_{2n-1}$. When this part of the register machine ${\mathcal{M}}$ is executed it will always create the same execution graph as seen in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:conp_init}. We refer to the final state of this initialization chain as $q_{init}$.
\par\begin{figure}\centering\hbox to297.91pt{\vbox to73.57pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-106.72803pt\lower 48.57251pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{14}}{Assignment section of ${\mathcal{M}}$ generated for a formula $\varphi=(x\wedge\overline{y})\vee(\overline{x}\wedge y)$. Note that $\varphi$ is not in 3-DNF form, but it is sufficient as an explanatory example. Assigning true to $x$ violates the second clause, so from $q_{x}^{T}$ we copy the register $b{2}$ into register $a{2}$. The other copy edges stem from similar violations.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{\small Figure 14}}{\small Assignment section of ${\mathcal{M}}$ generated for a formula $\varphi=(x\wedge\overline{y})\vee(\overline{x}\wedge y)$. Note that $\varphi$ is not in 3-DNF form, but it is sufficient as an explanatory example. Assigning true to $x$ violates the second clause, so from $q_{x}^{T}$ we copy the register $b{2}$ into register $a{2}$. The other copy edges stem from similar violations.}}}
\end{figure}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@The assignment phase, in which we construct,
for each variable $x$ occurring in $\varphi$,
two states $q_{x}$ and $q_{x}^{\prime}$, which we connect with two different paths.
The first of these paths start with an $\epsilon$-transition
$q_{x}\to q_{x}^{T}$, which corresponds to assigning true to $x$.
Similarly, the second of these paths start with an
$\epsilon$-transition $q_{x}\to q_{x}^{F}$,
which corresponds to assigning false to $x$.
Let $I\subseteq\left\{{0,\dots,n-1}\right\}$ be the indices of clauses
in $\varphi$ that contain $\overline{x}$. Each such clause is
unsatisfied by the assignment of true to $x$.
To mark this, we create a chain of copy transitions
$a_{{i}}:=b_{{i}}$ for each $i\in I$, starting from
$q_{x}^{T}$ and ending at $q_{x}^{\prime}$. Similarly,
let $J\subseteq\left\{{0,\dots,n-1}\right\}$ be the indexes of clauses in
$\varphi$ that contain $x$, and create a chain of copy transitions
$a_{{i}}:=b_{{i}}$ for each $i\in J$,
starting from $q_{x}^{F}$ and ending at $q_{x}^{\prime}$.
An example for the gadget for variables $x$ and $y$ can be seen in Fig. \ref{fig:app_conp_assign}.
For some arbitrary enumeration $x_{1},\dots,x_{m}$ of the variables of $\varphi$, we chain these assignment gadges with $\epsilon$-transitions.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@The final phase is the output phase, where the register machine performs $n$ output transitions, one for each clause $c_{i}$
\par$$q_{i}^{out}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{c_{i}},x,a_{c_{i}}\right)}}q_{i+1}^{out}$$\par\end{enumerate}\par The three different phases of the machine are connected with $\epsilon$ transitions for convenience.
\end{itemize}\par The claim is that if there exists an assignment for the variables of $\varphi$ which makes $\varphi$ not hold then there is an ${\mathtt{RA}}$ violation of this register machine. The main idea here is that if there existed an assignment that violates the formula, this would imply that this assignment violates each clause in $\varphi$ (remember that the formula is in 3DNF). From the construction we have guaranteed that iff a clause is violated from an assignment then we will locally get for this clause the execution graph described in Fig. \ref{fig:local_clause_violation}.
\par\par\begin{figure}\begin{center}\hbox to209.25pt{\vbox to130.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-138.08553pt\lower-8.33301pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
\end{center}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{15}}{for an assignment of variables that violates $c_{i}$ we get the above local execution graph.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{\small Figure 15}}{\small for an assignment of variables that violates $c_{i}$ we get the above local execution graph.}}}
\end{figure}\par\par\par\@proof[Proof of \coNP-hardness]
For the proof of the right implication we assume there exists an assignment that invalidates $\varphi$. Since this is an assignment on the variables we know that for some execution of ${\mathcal{M}}$ this exact assignment of variables will be explored. Since $\varphi$ is not satisfied this means that for each clause $c_{i}$ there existed a variable in it that occurred in the assignment in the opposite form from what was in the literal.
\par This means that for each clause the copy command from $b_{c_{i}}$ to $a_{c_{i}}$ will have occurred. Consequently for this run we will be getting the ${\mathtt{SRA}}$ violation due to the cycle as shown in Fig. \ref{fig:conp_cycle}.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{16}}{The cycle detected in a formula $\varphi$ that is not a tautology.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{\small Figure 16}}{\small The cycle detected in a formula $\varphi$ that is not a tautology.}}}
\end{figure}\par\par For the proof of the reverse implication we assume such a cycle. We therefore get that for each clause there must have existed a variable which caused the copying of $b_{c_{i}}$ to $a_{c_{i}}$. Consequently after performing the variable assignment phase this copying was triggered by some variable in each clause, which means that this assignment ``disagreed'' in some literal with each clause, and therefore invalidates the whole formula.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\NP-hardness}
The proof of {$\mathsf{NP}$}-hardness for the $\operatorname{\mathtt{RA-Cons}}$ is done from the boolean satisfiability problem (SAT) and is almost dual to the {$\mathsf{coNP}$}-hardness one. Given a boolean formula $\varphi$ over propositional variables $x,y,z,\dots$, we will construct, in polynomial time, a register machine ${\mathcal{M}}$ such that ${\mathcal{M}}\not\models{\mathtt{RA}}$ if and only if $\varphi$ is satisfiable. In this way we get that for any polynomial algorithm that detects a violation of the ${\mathtt{RA}}$, one can use the violating run to construct a satisfying assignment for a given boolean 3CNF formula.
\par Without loss of generality we assume $\varphi$ is in $3$-conjunctive normal form, and we will represent its clauses as $c_{0},c_{1},\ldots c_{n-1}$, and the literals occurring in a clause $i$ as $\ell_{i,1},\ell_{i,1},\text{ and }\ell_{i,3}$. Hence:
\par$$\varphi=c_{0}\wedge c_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge c_{n-1}=(\ell_{0,1}\vee\ell_{0,2}\vee\ell_{0,3})\wedge\ldots\wedge(\ell_{n-1,1}\vee\ell_{n-1,2}\vee\ell_{n-1,3})~.$$\par The reduction works as follows:
\par\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@Given the formula $\varphi$, we parse it and create a register machine over: \begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@The threads $\theta_{i}$ with one thread per clause of $\varphi$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@A single variable $x$, and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@the registers $a_{i}$ and $b_{i}$ corresponding to a clause $c_{i}$ being violated or satisfied.
\end{enumerate}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@We construct three phases in the register machine. The three phases are, in the order we describe:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@The initialization of clauses phase, where the register machine performs $2$ write events per clause $c_{i}$ of the formula $\varphi$, in a single path,
$$q_{2i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{i},x,b_{i-1\mod n},\right)}}q_{2i+1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{W}},\theta_{i},x,a_{i},\right)}}q_{2i+2}$$\par starting at state $q_{0}$, and ending in state $q_{2n-1}$. When this part of the register machine ${\mathcal{M}}$ is executed it will always create the same execution graph as seen in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:conp_init}. We refer to the final state of this initialization chain as $q_{init}$.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@The assignment phase, in which we construct, for each variable $x$ occurring in $\varphi$, two states $q_{x}$ and $q_{x}^{\prime}$, which we connect with two different paths. The first of these paths start with an $\epsilon$-transition $q_{x}\to q_{x}^{T}$, which corresponds to assigning true to $x$. Similarly, the second of these paths start with an $\epsilon$-transition $q_{x}\to q_{x}^{F}$, which corresponds to assigning false to $x$. Let $I\subseteq\left\{{0,\dots,n-1}\right\}$ be the indices of clauses in $\varphi$ that contain $x$. Each such clause is satisfied by the assignment of true to $x$. To mark this, we create a chain of copy transitions $a_{i}:=b_{i}$ for each $i\in I$, starting from $q_{x}^{T}$ and ending at $q_{x}^{\prime}$. Similarly, let $J\subseteq\left\{{0,\dots,n-1}\right\}$ be the indices of clauses in $\varphi$ that contain $\overline{x}$, and create a chain of copy transitions $a_{i}:=b_{i}$ for each $i\in J$, starting from $q_{x}^{F}$ and ending at $q_{x}^{\prime}$.
\par The gadget for variables $x$ and $y$ can be seen in Fig. \ref{fig:app_conp_assign}.
For some arbitrary enumeration $x_{1},\dots,x_{m}$ of the variables of $\varphi$, we chain these assignment gadgets with $\epsilon$-transitions.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@The final phase is the output phase, where the register machine performs $n$ output transitions, one for each clause $c_{i}$
\par$$q_{i}^{out}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\mathsf{R}},\theta_{i},x,a_{i}\right)}}q_{i+1}^{out}$$\par\end{enumerate}\par The three different phases of the machine are connected with $\epsilon$ transitions for convenience.
\end{itemize}\par The claim is that if there exists an assignment for the variables of $\varphi$ which makes $\varphi$ hold then there is an ${\mathtt{SRA}}$ violation of this register machine.
The proof of this construction is exactly dual to the one for {$\mathsf{coNP}$}.
